The Dock is collapsed by default (localStorage mm.v4.dock remembers the choice) and the scene takes the whole height; a tab with a chevron at the bottom edge (above the Safari 26 bar) unfolds it, the Dock slides over the bottom of the scene and the tab sits on its top edge (chevron down) to fold it back; folded, the Dock is inert. RESET VIEW moves to the top right, under the header. Rendering on the mobile tier: DPR cap 3 (1.5 before), antialias on, panel silkscreen and PCB textures at 2048 px, shadow map 1024: the machine looked pixelated on Mika's iPhone. The section sheet keeps its bottom above the Safari bar (--ios-bar).

R14-15. Portrait machine, trial (2026-10-01, theme PORTRAIT). On a phone, ?portrait=1 (kept for the tab in sessionStorage, ?portrait=0 forgets it) builds a tall MM-808 like a Traktor X1 or an SP-404, with the same parts placed differently: body 8.2 x 14.4; top to bottom the wordmark row, the OLED with MASTER and TEMPO side by side on its right, the transport row (pitch 1.3), the GLOBAL knobs on step columns 2 to 7, the pads (pitch 1.12) with OPEN in a sixth column, the VOICE knobs between the columns, then the 16 steps in two rows of 8 (pitch 0.86, second row 1.95 lower, keyX / keyDz). The 3D steps answer on the phone in portrait (about 40 px). The PCB is the original board turned a quarter (PCB_TURN), the three chips in a column on the right; the lid opens on a back hinge (60 deg, lift 6.3, slide -3.6) so the whole board shows. Back panel ports packed on 8.2, logo and sticker in the bottom band. Framing: FRAME_MOBILE 0.86, MACHINE_H 14.2, ORBIT.targetY 1.269, open view fitHalfH 11.5 and targetY 3.9. Without the flag, mobile and desktop are unchanged (measured).

R14-16. The portrait trial is forced by ?portrait=1 at any width (Mika first opened it on a computer and saw no change, the flag only worked under 768 px). On desktop the tall machine is height-fitted (775 px of 900 at 1440 x 900) and slides left of the section panel (SECTION_FRAME radius 8.3 closed, 10.3 open in portrait). Without the flag nothing changes.

R14-17. Portrait is the default on phones (2026-10-01, Mika: "fais en sorte que la machine change sur une resolution de telephone"). Under 768 px wide at load the tall machine is built; ?portrait=1 and ?portrait=0 force one or the other at any width (kept for the tab). Desktop unchanged. Measured at 390 x 844: steps, pads and knobs all 48 x 48 px targets.

R14-18. Mobile polish (2026-10-02). Machine open on the phone: a CLOSE button (ui/PcbClose.tsx, the orange of OPEN, 132 x 52 px) sits at the bottom of the screen over the front of the board, above the Dock tab; it appears once the machine is open and leaves as soon as it closes (the CLOSE pad is on the raised lid, far and foreshortened). Portrait knobs larger: MASTER and TEMPO x1.35, GLOBAL x1.25, VOICE x1.15 (collars measured 34, 33 and 31 px at 390 px wide), labels follow the collar edge. The section sheet no longer has the row of page tabs (the hamburger menu has them): handle, close button, then the open section and its title; the tab strip code and keepInRow are removed.

R14-19. Stronger effects (2026-10-02, Mika: "at 100% too subtle"). REVERB: impulse 2.4 s (1.2), pre-delay 20 ms, send at 1.0 (0.3). DELAY: send 0.9 (0.55), feedback 0.58 (0.42), low-pass 4.5 kHz. CHORUS: depth +/-7 ms (3.5), dry 1 - 0.5 v, wet 1.0 v (0.35 / 0.7). DIST: D = 1 + 12 d (5), mix 0.85 d (0.5). The same laws apply to the per-voice effects. Offline renders (no playback), one SD hit at 100 %: reverb tail (0.4 to 2 s) -61 to -42 dB, delay -40 to -34 dB, DIST changes the sound by +10 dB (+2). Output guard: a soft clipper at the end of the chain (identity below 0.9, tanh up to 1, inputs up to +/-4): the dry pattern still peaks at -0.8 dBFS untouched, all four effects at 100 % peak at 0.0 dBFS (+8.3 dB without it). A DynamicsCompressor limiter was tried and dropped: it let attacks through and raised the dry level with its automatic make-up gain.

R14-20. GLOBAL FX and VOICE FX zones (2026-10-02, Mika: the small GLOBAL caption was too subtle). Each knob row sits in its own silkscreen zone: a faint fill (SILK_ZONES, ink at 7 %, corners 0.12) and a frame around the knobs and their labels, open at the bottom centre for its name in bold, 0.1 cap (0.06 before): GLOBAL FX, VOICE FX. Same in the SVG fallback. Tracks and the global effects: the tracks play in the SoundCloud widget (a cross-origin iframe); the browser does not let the page touch that audio, so the global effects cannot be applied to them as long as they stream from SoundCloud.
Decision (2026-10-02): Mika keeps SoundCloud; the global effects apply to the drum machine only, not to the tracks.

R14-21. Desktop VOICE FX row under the page pads (2026-10-02, Mika: REVERB sat under OPEN). VOLUME under TRACKS, REVERB under CONTACT, the seven knobs evenly spread between them (pitch 0.667 instead of the 0.74 step grid); measured at 1440 x 900: VOLUME 748 px / TRACKS 748, REVERB 1051 / CONTACT 1046. The GLOBAL row stays on the step grid; portrait unchanged.

R14-22. The grey frames and tinted zones of R14-20 are removed (Mika: "grossier"). Under each knob row a thin bracket like the step brackets (ticks at both ends), broken in the middle by its name in bold, 0.1 cap: GLOBAL FX, VOICE FX. SILK_ZONES and its drawing (silk.ts, SVG fallback) are gone.

R14-23. Desktop step sequencer 0.11 lower (2026-10-02, Mika: the steps touched the GLOBAL FX / VOICE FX names): KEYS.z 2.51, ledZ 1.73, numberZ 3.19, bracketZ 3.36 on the landscape machine (portrait unchanged); measured 8.2 px lower at 1440 x 900 (about 75 px per unit there, foreshortened by the 69 deg view).

R14-24. SEO (2026-10-02). Each section has its URL: /tracks/, /mixtapes/, /shows/, /contact/, /goodies/, /merch/, /studio/ (React routes to the machine, src/v4/state/sectionRoute.ts): landing on one opens that section once the intro is done (the PCB sections open the machine first); opening or closing a section updates the URL with pushState (GA4 page views, back button) and the tab title (from src/data/seo-meta.json). PRESS keeps the home URL (/press/ is the static press assets page, /presskit/ the viewer). Build (scripts/prerender-seo.mjs): every route gets its static page with title, description, canonical, Open Graph and its content in plain HTML inside #root (bio, upcoming and past shows, festivals and venues, tracks and catalogue, mixtapes, contacts, site links), visually hidden and replaced by React on mount, readable by crawlers without JavaScript (Bing, social cards, AI assistants); upcoming dates (events.json, date >= build day) as schema.org MusicEvent JSON-LD on / and /shows/. Sitemap: 12 URLs. sameAs: Beatport added. Stickers: /qr/ redirects to /?utm_source=sticker&utm_medium=qr&utm_campaign=stickers (noindex); QR files in docs/stickers/ (error correction H, decoded back to https://mauditemachine.com/qr/).

R14-25. Shuffled tracks (2026-10-03, Mika: the playlist always started from the same track). TRACKS lists the 37 releases in a random order drawn at each visit (Fisher-Yates in src/v4/data.ts); the SoundCloud queue follows the displayed order. MIXTAPES unchanged.

R14-26. Two machines: the MM-VOYAGER (2026-10-03, behind ?voyager=1 until Mika validates it; ?voyager=0 forgets it, kept for the tab). A second machine on the same table, right of the MM-808 (VOY_X 15.7 desktop, 10.2 portrait), Minimoog Voyager spirit in the MM-808 design language: walnut cheeks (procedural grain texture), matte black panel (cream white on the light machine) rising to the back, Moog knobs (aluminium skirt, fluted black cap, white index; CUTOFF and VOLUME larger with 0 to 10 ticks), sections framed like a Moog panel (ARPEGGIATOR, OSCILLATORS, FILTER, FILTER EG, AMP EG, EFFECTS, OUTPUT), a front deck where pads replace the keyboard. Desktop 13.6 x 8.6; on phones a tall version 8.6 x 14.4 with the same parts (6 knob columns in 4 rows, chord pads 4 x 2). Same links as the MM-808 (TRACKS to CONTACT buttons with orange labels, OPEN that breathes); OPEN lifts the lid (deck and panel as one, scene/explode.ts with its own ExplodeCfg) and shows the same PCB (MM-VOYAGER REV 1.0 silkscreen) with GOODIES, MERCH and STUDIO. Files: src/v4/voyager/ (theme, params, chords, arp, body, knobs, pads, silk, lcd, msg, rig), src/v4/audio/synth.ts, src/v4/state/focus.ts, ui/VoyTwins.tsx, ui/MachineNav.tsx.

R14-27. The synth (audio/synth.ts). One note: two oscillators of the same shape (WAVE morphs saw, square, 12 % pulse, nine periodic waves), FINE spreads them apart on both sides of the note (0 to 28 cents, the centre stays in tune: never out of key), GLIDE from the previous note; 24 dB low-pass (a flat Butterworth stage and a resonant one, RES -3 to +20 dB, half the resonance gain taken back), CUTOFF 30 Hz to 19 kHz with half keyboard tracking, FILTER EG (ATTACK DECAY SUSTAIN RELEASE) opening 0 to 6 octaves (ENV AMT), AMP EG. Notes meet on a bus: DIST (the drum machine saturation law) then CHORUS (its own: three slow voices, depth 2.4 ms, wide stereo; on by default at 40 %, Mika wants it present) then VOLUME into the drum machine compressor (drums.synthPort: same analyser, soft clipper and master, ?mute=1 holds); REVERB and DELAY are sends to the drum machine units. Output makeup for DIST, DELAY and REVERB. Offline renders (no playback), F#m arpeggio 1/16 at 130 BPM: default peak -7 dBFS, RMS -23; every effect at 100 % peak -2 dBFS, RMS -16. All 23 knobs persist in mm.v4.voyager.1.

R14-28. The arpeggiator (voyager/arp.ts). Eight chord pads in F sharp minor (F#m, D, E, C#m, Bm, A, F#m7, Dmaj7): every note is in the key whatever is touched. A tapped pad joins the progression (tap order, up to 8), tapped again it leaves; one chord per bar; the first chord starts the arpeggio, no chord stops it. RATE 1/4 1/8 1/16 1/32, MODE UP DOWN UP/DN RAND, RANGE 1 to 3 octaves, GATE. RANDOM picks a ready-made progression (never the one playing), CLEAR empties it. Same scheduler as the drum clock (25 ms wake-up, 100 ms lookahead on the audio clock); while the MM-808 runs, each step is taken on its grid (clock.gridAfter): bars and tempo locked, its SWING applied to the arpeggio; light accents (the "a" of each beat forward, like Mika's bass). One source at a time like RUN: a SoundCloud track that starts stops the arpeggio, a chord tapped during a track pauses it. The playing chord's pad is yellowHi and flashes on each note, queued pads are orange; the LCD shows ARP 1/16 UP 2 OCT, the progression (playing chord in negative) and the knob being turned. Keyboard: A S D F G H J K play the chords while the Voyager is in use.

R14-29. Focus and framing (scene/renderer.ts, state/focus.ts). focus: 'all' (overview), 'mm808' or 'voy'. Each target has its frame (pivot x, half width, projected height, pivot closed and open, section radii, open stack, shadow extent); a change animates between frames in 900 ms (easeInOutCubic), the view comes back to the front, the key light, back light and its shadow camera follow the target, the yellow rim stays on the left of the machine in use. Once arrived, only the machine in use is shown (orbiting never puts the other one in front of it) and only its objects answer (HitMap.setActive); the floor draws a contact shadow per visible machine and fogs from the nearest one. The intro opens both machines and closes them together in the overview. Desktop: the overview stays; under each machine its name (MM-808 DRUM MACHINE, MM-VOYAGER SYNTHESIZER, a button); a click on a machine zooms on it; BOTH MACHINES (bottom left), Escape and the logo come back. Phone: after the intro the MM-808 settles; a horizontal one-finger swipe (over 56 px, 1.4 times more horizontal than vertical, under 700 ms, not from a knob) goes to the other machine; a MM-808 / VOYAGER switch under the header and a chevron at the screen edge do the same; the Dock (MM-808 sequencer) only shows on the MM-808. OPEN, GOODIES, MERCH, STUDIO and the phone CLOSE act on the machine in use (the MM-808 in the overview). Measured: 12 draw calls with one machine shown, 18 at most in the overview.

R14-30. MM-VOYAGER, second pass after Mika's test (2026-10-03). Name: MM-VOYAGER (not VOYAGEUR) everywhere (silk, LCD, PCB, switch, labels, storage mm.v4.voyager.1). Engine replaced by an AudioWorklet (audio/moog.worklet.js, loaded at the first gesture, plain JS emitted as an asset): per note three oscillators (OSC 1 and 2 spread by FINE up to 45 cents around the note, OSC 3 one octave down), continuous Voyager waveform (triangle, saw, square, 12 % pulse) with PolyBLEP, slow per-oscillator drift; 24 dB ladder filter (four TPT one-poles, zero-delay feedback solved per sample, tanh at its input, resonance up to self-oscillation, bass loss partly compensated), half keyboard tracking; capacitor-curve ADSRs retriggered from their level; GLIDE in log pitch (up to 0.6 s); up to 12 overlapping voices; DIST drives the filter input and saturates the VCA output (level compensated). Default WAVE is the saw (1/3). New knob OCTAVE (-2 to +2) in OSCILLATORS (desktop: OCTAVE and GLIDE under WAVE and FINE; portrait: a PITCH section in row 2). Effects: CHORUS is a Juno-106 style chorus (two BBD-like delay lines around 3.5 ms modulated in anti-phase by one slow triangle, 8 kHz low-pass on the wet path, wet full from 30 % of the knob, mode I to mode II rate, plus the fast I+II shimmer above 75 %); DELAY is the synth's own stereo ping-pong (dotted eighth on the tempo, feedback 0.35 to 0.68, filtered loop); REVERB sends 1.25x into the shared reverb. Offline renders (no playback), F#m arpeggio at 130 BPM: dry peak -5 dBFS; brightness follows CUTOFF from about 200 to 1300 Hz, ENV AMT 356 to 668 Hz, filter DECAY 383 to 959 Hz, RES up to 1300 Hz; DELAY at 100 % leaves a -27 dB tail, REVERB a -32 dB wash; every effect at 100 % peaks at -3.6 dBFS.

R14-31. Wood, desktop machine drawer, PCBs, phone (2026-10-03). Wood: procedural quarter-sawn grain (straight rings, fibres, pores, slow tone), bump map, satin clearcoat reflecting a soft studio: smoked walnut on the dark machine, white oak on the light one; cheek profile with a generous nose, a soft fillet at the panel bend and rounded edges. Desktop: a machine drawer on the left edge (the mouse at the edge or the MACHINES tab slides it in 200 ms): BOTH MACHINES, MM-808 and MM-VOYAGER with live 3D thumbnails rendered by the scene (Stage.thumbnail, drawn in a canvas corner and copied in the same task), the current one outlined in orange; it replaces the BOTH MACHINES button. PCBs: glossy solder mask (MeshPhysicalMaterial clearcoat reflecting the board environment) over a relief normal map drawn like the colour (copper, pads and silkscreen raised), greener mask, denser routing; MM-808 board gets SOT-23 transistors, test points, fiducials and a barcode sticker; the MM-VOYAGER board is analogue: blue trimmers, red film capacitors, TO-92 transistors, DIP op-amps (TL074, LM13700), audio chips (TL072, CA3046). Phone: the MM-VOYAGER has its own collapsible Dock (eight chord buttons, CLEAR, RANDOM, OCTAVE -/+, RATE, MODE, RANGE). Desktop: a window crossing 768 px for 600 ms reloads into the right machine shape (fine pointers only, never on a phone, not when ?portrait forces it).

R14-32. The MM-VOYAGER is on for everyone (2026-10-03, Mika: "active le pour tout le monde"): VOYAGER defaults to true; ?voyager=0 removes it for the tab, ?voyager=1 brings it back.

R14-33. The other machine peeks (desktop, 2026-10-03, Mika). When one machine is in use, the other one stays on the table at the screen edge (right of the MM-808, left of the MM-VOYAGER): it is moved so that its inner edge falls 36 px from the edge at the pivot plane, whatever the window width (never closer than 0.8 units to the machine in use), and slides with the zoom from one machine to the other. Hovered, it comes out 40 px more in 180 ms with a pointer cursor; a click zooms on it. It hides as soon as the view is turned (it would pass in front) and comes back with RESET VIEW; with a section open it sits under the panel. The floor contact shadows follow the machines (uniform positions). Phone unchanged: one machine at a time, swipe. The MACHINES drawer button moves to the bottom left (a pill), the left-edge hover still opens the drawer.

R14-34. MM-VOYAGER layout made readable (2026-10-03, Mika: "un trop gros amas de boutons, on n'arrive pas a separer les choses"; "enleve les boutons du site et mets-les dans OPEN, en mode PCB"; "ne touche pas au mm808"; "je veux aussi le logo Type sur le Voyager"). The five page buttons leave the deck: TRACKS, MIXTAPES, SHOWS, PRESS and CONTACT become medium chips on the Voyager board (VOY_PAGE_CHIPS, CHIP_MID 1.2 x 0.86, orange labels, a row at board z 0.17 between the analogue parts and GOODIES / MERCH / STUDIO, shifted left so the phone's open lid never hides CONTACT); the band they sit in is cleared of SMD resistors, ceramics and the coin cell, the ground pour stops lower, MAUDITE MACHINE moves behind the band and MM-VOYAGER / R1.0 2026 to the right of the chips. They act like the MM-808 page pads (CONTACT drafts Booking, PRESS reopens the viewer from /presskit). The MM-808 board is unchanged (same chips, parts, silk, refs). Desktop open lid goes higher and further (lift 5.0, slide -3.8, tilt -24 deg) so the page row is fully visible; open camera radius 9.6. Deck: LCD, RUN/STOP, CLEAR, RANDOM, then OPEN on the right; the eight pads centred under a bracket CHORDS F# MINOR. Panel header like the MM-808: wordmark, MM-VOYAGER next to it, the logotype (mark) on the right (desktop: ARPEGGIATOR SYNTHESIZER before it). Phone (8.6 x 14.4): the panel bend moves back (bendZ -1.2) and keeps only the sound, three rows of two groups, each underlined by a named bracket like the MM-808 rows: OSCILLATORS (WAVE FINE GLIDE) / FILTER (CUTOFF RES ENV AMT), FILTER EG (A D S R) / COLOR (DIST CHORUS), AMP EG (A D S R) / SPACE (DELAY REVERB). The deck holds the LCD and VOLUME, the transport row (RUN/STOP CLEAR RANDOM OPEN, aligned on the pads), the ARPEGGIATOR row (RATE MODE RANGE GATE OCTAVE) and the 4 x 2 pads. Knobs now live on the lid (panel knobs through the fixed panel pose), each with its plane for picking and labels.

R14-35. RUN/STOP on the MM-VOYAGER, tempo shared with the MM-808 (2026-10-03, Mika: "quand l'un ou l'autre est clique les deux se synchronisent niveau tempo"). RUN/STOP stops or restarts the arpeggio without touching the progression (empty: it starts on F#m); its key stays yellowHi while it plays (like the MM-808 RUN lamp). Stopped, the pads compose the progression without restarting it (only the first chord of an empty progression starts it); RANDOM starts it; CLEAR stops it. Space plays RUN/STOP of the machine in use; the phone Dock gets a RUN/STOP key. Both machines run on one tempo (the MM-808 TEMPO) and one grid: the arpeggio started while the drums play takes their grid (clock.gridAfter); drums started while the arpeggio plays join its grid (clock.follow, the arpeggiator registers its next step boundary and number): first drum step on an arpeggio note, same 16-step position, swing included (measured: 0 ms). A SoundCloud track that starts now stops the arpeggio and keeps the progression. The LCD shows the tempo on its first line (130 BPM), a play triangle while it runs, and RUN/STOP TO PLAY when stopped with chords.

R14-36. Phone CLOSE above the Dock (2026-10-03, Mika). With a machine open, the orange CLOSE no longer covers an unfolded Dock: it rises to the Dock height (read from the DOM, the MM-808 Dock or the MM-VOYAGER one, followed with a MutationObserver on data-open and a ResizeObserver) plus 36 px (the tab and some air), in 260 ms like the tab, and comes back down when the Dock folds. Measured at 375 x 812: CLOSE bottom 601 px over the tab at 612 (MM-808), 591 over 612 (MM-VOYAGER); folded: back at 760.

R14-37. RANDOM draws the effects, NOTES sets the arpeggio length (2026-10-03, Mika: "que RANDOM fasse du random sur les FX sur les machines", "le choix du nombre de notes dans l'arp"). RANDOM on the MM-808 (deck key, Dock) also sets the bus effects: DIST off 55 % of the time else 0.05 to 0.35, CHORUS off 50 % else 0.1 to 0.5, DELAY off 40 % else 0.1 to 0.4, REVERB 0.05 to 0.4 (SWING, STRETCH, MASTER and the voice effects untouched); the LCD says RANDOM HOUSE + FX. RANDOM on the MM-VOYAGER also sets DIST (off 50 % else 0.08 to 0.4), CHORUS 0.25 to 0.85, DELAY (off 35 % else 0.15 to 0.55), REVERB 0.1 to 0.5; the LCD says RANDOM + FX. New stepped knob NOTES (ALL, 1 to 8) in ARPEGGIATOR: ALL plays the whole sequence (chord tones over RANGE octaves in MODE order, as before); N plays its first N notes and starts again (wrapping past its end), so 3 or 5 notes turn against the bar; the LCD adds 5N while it runs (line 1 shrinks to keep the tempo readable). The phone Dock gets a NOTES key. Desktop: the ARPEGGIATOR row (RATE MODE RANGE NOTES GATE, under its bracket) moves from the panel to the deck, left of the LCD; the panel keeps the sound in wider columns (OSCILLATORS, FILTER, FILTER EG / AMP EG, EFFECTS, OUTPUT), RUN/STOP CLEAR RANDOM sit right of the LCD, OPEN at the right end. Phone: the ARPEGGIATOR row has six knobs (RATE MODE RANGE NOTES GATE OCTAVE).

R14-38. MM-808: MUTE mode, pages on the board, ten voices (2026-10-03, Mika: "quand on est sur MUTE on clique une voix pour la muter, une autre aussi ; on reclique MUTE et elles se demutent" ; "les boutons TRACKS, MIXTAPES, SHOWS... dans OPEN, et occupe l'espace pour d'autres voix parametrables, prends exemple sur une Rytm"). MUTE is now a latched mode: its lamp stays lit; while it is on, every voice pad (and its Dock button, and its key) mutes or unmutes its voice without playing it, as many as wanted (the pads turn powder pink); MUTE again leaves the mode and brings every voice back. SOLO unchanged. The five page pads leave the panel: TRACKS to CONTACT become the same medium chips on the MM-808 board as on the MM-VOYAGER (PAGE_CHIPS, BOARD_CHIPS for both boards; the band is cleared of SMD resistors, ceramics and the coin cell on both, the ground pour stops lower, MAUDITE MACHINE moves behind the band, MM-808 / REV 4.0 2026 right of the chips), keys 1 to 5 still open them; the desktop lid opens higher (lift 5.0, slide -3.9, tilt -24 deg, open target 5.9) so the row shows. Their place on the panel takes five new voices in the Analog Rytm naming, each under its neighbour: CP (clap: band-passed noise struck three times 10 ms apart, then a 220 ms tail), RS (rimshot: two short inharmonic triangles, 1.7 kHz and 455 Hz, 70 ms), HT (high tom: the TOM a fifth up, shorter), CY (cymbal: the six 808 metallic squares an octave up, band-passed at 8 kHz, a noise veil, 1.1 s), CB (cowbell: 540 and 800 Hz squares band-passed at 2.6 kHz, quick drop then 380 ms). Keys Z X C V B. Each has the full VOICE FX strip (VOLUME TONE STRETCH DIST CHORUS DELAY REVERB) and its own sequencer row; the arrival pattern keeps the new rows empty. Offline single-hit levels (no playback): CP -7.4 dBFS peak, RS -4.3, HT -3.9, CY -7.4, CB -5.5 (BD -4.6, SD -6.7). RANDOM: the clap moves to CP; SD doubles it on 2 and 4 or slips ghosts; RS, HT, CY and CB are colours, often absent (a syncopated rim, a high tom roll at the bar end, a crash on the 1 or a soft ride, a cowbell figure). The MIX page shows the five levels of the selected voice's row. Phone Dock: RANDOM over two rows, the ten voices five per row; its tab sits on the taller Dock (209 px; the MM-VOYAGER Dock keeps 185 px).

R14-39. Knob skirts (2026-10-03, Mika: "les strokes des knobs du Voyager un peu moins larges et un peu plus fonces en dark ; rajoute ce genre de stroke dans la 808"). MM-VOYAGER: the aluminium skirt narrows to r 0.31 (top 0.295; 0.35 / 0.33 before), a thin ring around the cap; on the dark machine its tone goes from #A7ABB2 to #80848B (light machine unchanged, #CDD0D6). MM-808: the dark collar under each encoder becomes the same bevelled aluminium skirt (r 0.32, top 0.302, 0.05 high, voySkirt tone, roughness 0.42 metalness 0.28), a second static InstancedMesh (one more draw call); positions, labels and picking unchanged.

R14-40. Thinner skirts, second pass (2026-10-03, Mika: "encore plus fin, surtout pour le Voyager ; plus fonce en dark, plus clair en light, pour les deux machines"). MM-VOYAGER skirt r 0.284, top 0.272, 0.04 high (a thin rim around the 0.25 cap); MM-808 skirt r 0.298, top 0.287, 0.04 high (ENCODER.skirt; ENCODER.collar keeps r 0.32 for the label and bracket layout). Tone voySkirt, both machines: dark #5D6167 (#80848B in R14-39), light #E6E8EC (#CDD0D6).

R14-41. The second machine is called MM-ARP; steady chord pad (2026-10-03, Mika: "le titre c'est MM-808 et l'autre c'est MM-ARP" ; "ca clignote quand je clique dessus"). Every visible name of the second machine becomes MM-ARP: panel silkscreen, LCD idle line, PCB silkscreen (MM-ARP / R1.0 2026) and chip markings, phone switch (MM-808 / MM-ARP) and edge chevron label, the name under it in the overview (MM-ARP SYNTHESIZER), the desktop drawer, twin group name. Internal names stay (voy, VOYAGER flag, ?voyager=0, mm.v4.voyager.1, src/v4/voyager/). The playing chord's pad no longer flashes on every arpeggio note (it blinked eight times a second at 1/16): it stays yellowHi, steady; a tapped pad still flashes once.

R14-42. Advanced SEO (2026-10-03, Mika: "du referencement pousse"). Audit (Lighthouse 12, live, mobile): SEO 100, accessibility 100, best practices 79, performance 28 (FCP 6.7 s, LCP 10.1 s, TBT 3.2 s; 2 s lost on render-blocking third-party CSS). Done:
1. One page per track: /tracks/<id>/ for the 37 releases of src/v2/data/discography.json (route /tracks/:track in App.tsx; sectionFromPath maps it to TRACKS, which opens; the address and the static title stay). Each page: title, description (facts only: VRSTL Records named only for the press kit catalogue and the Limbos tracks; "unofficial edit, free download" only for the four edits of the press kit), canonical, Open Graph music.song, readable content (facts, Bandcamp and SoundCloud links, six related tracks, back to /tracks/), JSON-LD MusicRecording (byArtist #artist, datePublished, inAlbum Limbos, sameAs) and BreadcrumbList. /tracks/ lists every track page (internal links) with an ItemList.
2. French and Spanish home pages /fr/ and /es/: real, light pages (no 3D, no app bundle) in the site look (cream, ink, orange, SF Pro Display), translated from the press kit without additions: bio, the sound and the two formats, upcoming shows (dates in the language), festivals, latest releases (links to the track pages), Mixtape 39, platforms, booking, press kit and tech rider, a button into the machine. hreflang en / fr / es / x-default on the three home pages and in the sitemap; ProfilePage JSON-LD; Lighthouse 100 performance, 100 accessibility, 100 SEO.
3. Entity graph: MusicGroup @id https://mauditemachine.com/#artist (logo, album Limbos), WebSite @id #website (about and publisher: the artist); every page references the artist by @id; section pages carry a BreadcrumbList.
4. Section titles: an address of a section or a track keeps the title of its static page when the app mounts (Google reads the rendered title).
5. Sitemap: 51 URLs (track pages, /fr/, /es/ with their alternates).
6. Render-blocking third-party CSS removed: Font Awesome and the fonts of the archived pages (moved out of styles.css) load with media=print then all; LightWidget is deferred. Local build: no third-party render-blocking resource left.
7. robots.txt allows the AI search crawlers (OAI-SearchBot, ChatGPT-User, Claude-SearchBot, Google-Extended, Applebot-Extended); /llms.txt summarises the artist, the facts, booking and the key pages for assistants.
Not done (needs Mika or a decision): Search Console and Bing Webmaster verification and sitemap submission; IndexNow pings on deploy; Knowledge Panel sources (Wikidata, MusicBrainz, Discogs, Resident Advisor); the 3D bundle itself (LCP bound by JavaScript on mobile).

R14-43. MM-ARP: two oscillators, Dreadbox Typhon style, waveforms drawn around the selectors (2026-10-03, Mika). Engine (audio/moog.worklet.js): two oscillators per note instead of three. OSC 1 WAVE 1, six steps: sine, triangle, saw, square, 14 % pulse, FM (its sine phase-modulated by OSC 2, about 1.6 rad); OSC 2 WAVE 2, five steps (the same without FM); a step change crossfades in 10 ms (no click). TUNE 2 tunes OSC 2 in musical steps, always in key: -1 octave (default, the former sub), unison, fifth, +1, +2 octaves. MIX balances the two at constant power (0.62 each in the middle, 0.88 alone). FINE still spreads them around the note. Offline renders (no playback): every wave has its own level and spectrum, FM doubles the zero crossings, TUNE 2 measures x0.5, x1, x1.5, x4. Panel: around WAVE 1 and WAVE 2, one Typhon-yellow arc per step (amber on the light machine) with the drawing of its wave outside (FM written); the chosen step full, the others dimmed; the panel silkscreen redraws when a step changes. Desktop OSCILLATORS on three columns: WAVE 1, WAVE 2, TUNE 2 over FINE, MIX, GLIDE; section titles move up (z -1.6). OCTAVE joins the ARPEGGIATOR row on the deck (six knobs), the LCD and transport move right. Phone: the panel gets four rows (bend at z -0.5, panel knobs x1.15): OSCILLATORS (WAVE 1, WAVE 2, TUNE 2, MIX); FILTER and COLOR; FILTER EG and SPACE; AMP EG and PITCH (FINE, GLIDE); the deck is tightened (LCD and VOLUME, transport, ARPEGGIATOR, pads 1.1).

R14-44. MM-ARP phone Dock: WAVE 1 and WAVE 2 (2026-10-03, Mika). A new Dock row with two keys: the name, the drawing of the current wave (the same drawings as around the selectors, voyager/glyphs.ts, Typhon yellow; amber on the light machine; FM written) and its name; a tap goes to the next wave (WAVE 1: sine, triangle, saw, square, pulse, FM; WAVE 2 without FM), the machine's selector and the LCD follow. The Dock is 230 px high (its tab sits on its top edge, --vdock-h-mobile).

R14-45. Sound quality, the percussion, the panel cross (2026-10-03, Mika: "je veux et j'exige que le son sortant soit de la meilleure qualite qu'il soit" ; "remplace Cowbell" ; "la croix fait ouvrir le lien BUY").
1. Output limiter (audio/limiter.worklet.js): a lookahead peak limiter replaces the soft clipper at the end of the chain (analyser -> limiter -> master): required gain per sample (ceiling -1 dBFS over the peak), minimum over a 3 ms lookahead window, instant attack and 120 ms release, a moving average over the same window (a smooth ramp, the peak held exactly under the ceiling), the sound delayed to match; stereo linked. Loaded in the background on the first gesture; the soft clipper (now 4x oversampled) stays as the fallback. Measured offline: a 0 dBFS sine comes out at -1.00 dB peak and -4.01 dB RMS (exactly 1 dB less, no distortion); the arrival pattern is untouched (peak -1.66 dB before and after); all four effects at 100 % (up to +12 dB before the output) are held at -1.15 dB without clipping.
2. MM-ARP engine oversampled: the whole engine runs at 4x the context rate on computers, 2x on phones (processorOptions.os), and comes back through cascaded Kaiser half-band filters (31 then 63 taps, about -80 dB). Measured on a saw at 740 and 2093 Hz (inharmonic energy against harmonics): without DIST -32/-36 dB at 1x, -40/-42 at 2x, -45/-46 at 4x; DIST at 100 % -21/-26 at 1x, -26/-31 at 2x, -33/-38 at 4x (about 12 dB cleaner); cost about 3 % of real time per voice at 4x on a laptop.
3. The BD saturation is oversampled 4x (2x before). The parallel DIST stages stay without oversampling (their filter latency would comb against the dry path).
4. PC replaces CB (the cowbell): a conga, a tuned skin (sine, pitch falling a quarter in 25 ms), a short second mode (x1.5) and the hand slap (band-passed noise at 2.2 kHz, 12 ms), 200 ms; key B; RANDOM gives it soft syncopated figures four times out of ten. Offline: -4.4 dBFS peak, between the toms and the rimshot.
5. Section panel, desktop: the phone sheet handle (invisible, 44 px) sat in the panel head and pushed the close cross onto the first row, over its BUY link (which won the click); the handle is hidden off phones, the cross sits back level with the section title (10 to 54 px, the first BUY from 71 px) and the head is above the row links (z-index 5).
6. The AudioWorklet modules are never inlined as data: URLs by the build (vite.config.ts assetsInlineLimit): the 4 KB limiter would have been, and Safari may refuse a data: module in addModule.

R14-46. PC lower (2026-10-03, Mika: "le son de la PC est trop aigu"). The conga skin goes from 330 Hz to 200 Hz (measured offline: 200 Hz), the hand slap band from 2.2 kHz to 1.6 kHz, output trimmed by 1.2 dB to keep its peak with the other voices (about -4 dBFS).

R14-47. Waveform rings on the light machine (2026-10-03, Mika did not like the amber): the arcs and wave drawings use the silkscreen ink (dimmed to 50 % for the steps not chosen) and the chosen step is orange (#FF6A13), the site accent; the dark machine keeps the Typhon yellow. The phone Dock draws its wave in orange (#D9560E) on the light machine.

R14-48. RS lower; MM-808 RANDOM leaves the effects alone (2026-10-03, Mika: "RS sonne trop aigu" ; "le random ne devrait pas toucher aux FX GLOBAL ni aux FX VOICE"). The rimshot's two triangles go from 1.7 kHz and 455 Hz to 980 Hz and 290 Hz, its high-pass from 320 Hz to 180 Hz (offline: -4.8 dBFS peak). RANDOM on the MM-808 (deck key, Dock) draws the pattern only again: GLOBAL FX and VOICE FX stay as set (the LCD says RANDOM HOUSE; R14-37 point 1 for the MM-808 is withdrawn). RANDOM on the MM-ARP still draws its own effects (R14-37).

R14-49. MM-ARP RUN/STOP like the MM-808; a full rear panel (2026-10-03, Mika: "en light je veux la meme couleur RUN/STOP dans ARP" ; "le derriere de MM-ARP doit etre beaucoup plus evolue, comme MM-808").
1. RUN/STOP: the key takes the MM-808 RUN albedo (LIT.run, divided by the rubber's own colour, both appearances) and its lamp: a bar on the back of the key (BTN_LED size), the line colour when stopped, yellowHi while the arpeggio runs (it follows the key when pressed); the key itself no longer glows.
2. Rear panel (VOY_BACK, voyager/body.ts and voyager/backplate.ts), in the MM-808 manner: upper row CV IN (PITCH, CUTOFF, RES, VOLUME, WAVE, mini-jacks), GATE IN and OUT (mini-jacks), PEDALS EXP and SUS (6.35 jacks), EXT IN; lower row PHONES, MAIN OUT L and R, SYNC IN and OUT, MIDI IN, OUT and THRU (5-pin DIN with key), USB-C, DC 12V, POWER rocker; four Phillips screws (two on the phone), ventilation slots (dark in both appearances). Silkscreen: the wordmark, MM-ARP ARPEGGIATOR SYNTHESIZER, FIRMWARE V.1.0 / 2026, the serial sticker (barcode, S/N MMARP-000001), each port's name above it and each group under a bracket with its bold title; redrawn when fonts and logos arrive. Desktop and phone layouts.

R14-50. MM-ARP desktop logotype raised (2026-10-03, Mika: "trop colle a OUTPUT"): the mark goes up to z -2.05 (its own height, the header text stays at -1.98) and shrinks to 0.34 (0.44): about 0.24 of air above the OUTPUT title instead of 0.12. Phone unchanged.

R14-51. MM-ARP FM synthesis (2026-10-03, Mika: "de la synthese FM comme le Typhon"): an FM knob (0 to 1, off by default) in OSCILLATORS. OSC 2 phase-modulates OSC 1 whatever their waves; index up to 6 (FM^2 curve), shaped by the filter envelope (35 % at rest, 100 % at the envelope peak), so notes open bright and settle. TUNE 2 sets the ratio (0, 5th, octaves). Desktop: FM takes GLIDE's place (OSC column 3, row 2), GLIDE joins the deck ARPEGGIATOR (7 knobs, pitch 0.75), LCD and buttons move 0.45 to 0.55 right. Phone: FM is the 5th knob of the OSC row (TUNE 2 0.85, MIX 1.95, FM 3.05); Dock: an FM key next to WAVE 1 and WAVE 2 (OFF, 25, 50, 75, 100 %). Offline (no playback): saw, open filter, TUNE 2 +1 oct, centroid 2.39 kHz -> 3.59 kHz at FM 100 %, RMS unchanged (-14.8 dB), no NaN.

R14-52. MM-ARP FM RATIO (2026-10-03, Mika: "un ratio FM reglable comme le Typhon"): the FM source becomes a dedicated sine operator (its own phase per voice) at RATIO x OSC 1, RATIO stepped 1/2 1 3/2 2 3 7/2 4 5 7 (default 1), harmonic ratios so the sound stays in key. Index capped so (index + 1) x operator frequency stays under 0.4 x the internal rate. OSC 2 no longer feeds the FM knob (it still drives the WAVE 1 FM step). Desktop: OSC lower row FINE MIX FM RATIO at pitch 0.8 (-5.35 to -2.95). Phone: OSC row WAVE 1 -2.55, WAVE 2 -0.7, TUNE 2 0.575, MIX 1.425, FM 2.275, RATIO 3.125 (the right column). Dock: waves row in 4 keys (WAVE 1, WAVE 2, FM, RATIO), label over value, dock height unchanged (230 px). Offline (no playback), sine carrier, FM 100 %, open filter: centroid 189 Hz (off), 285 (1/2, f/2 subharmonic +5 dB), 496 (1), 596 (3/2), 1184 (3), 2824 (7); RMS within 1 dB; off-harmonic energy -44.5 dB with FM vs -45.4 dB without at 1480 Hz (no added aliasing, OS 2 and 4).

R14-53. MM-ARP NOISE and SLOPE (2026-10-03, Mika: "un bouton noise dans filter et un bouton 16db 24db pour switcher la pente", read as 12/24 dB). NOISE (0 to 1, default 0): white noise per voice (xorshift, a new draw per context sample held through oversampling, same level at OS 2 and 4) mixed into the filter input with the oscillators, level = NOISE^2 (full: about the level of one oscillator); it sounds only while a note plays (voice filter and VCA). SLOPE (switch, 12 dB / 24 dB, default 24 dB): output after two ladder stages or four (the feedback stays the 4-pole one), 15 ms crossfade, 12 dB output scaled 1 / (1 + 0.12 k) for the stronger resonance bump at stage 2. Switch = a small knob (0.62) with a 90 deg throw, ticks and '12' / '24' silk at 10:30 and 1:30, hit target widened by its marks; a tap toggles it, the wheel moves stepped MM-ARP knobs one detent per notch (it never moved them before: 2 % steps were rounded away). Desktop FILTER column: SLOPE under the title (-1.95, -1.05), CUTOFF up to z -0.05, NOISE on row 2 (0.85); RES and ENV AMT unchanged. Phone FILTER row: CUTOFF RES ENV AMT NOISE (split 4) and SLOPE at x 1.08, label aligned with the row. Offline (no playback): 8-16 kHz vs 1-2 kHz, -44 dB at 12 dB vs -77 dB at 24 dB, same RMS (-14.2 dB, res 0.9: -16.4 / -16.3); noise band 1-4 kHz 28 / 40 / 52 dB at 25 / 50 / 100 %, OS 2 and OS 4 within 0.2 dB; no NaN.

R14-54. The drum machine is named MM-RYTM (2026-10-03, Mika): tabs, drawer, panel header, LCD idle line, back plate (MM-RYTM DRUM MACHINE, S/N MMRYTM-000808), PCB silk, aria labels, static fallback, llms.txt. Internal ids (mm808) unchanged. Shows: Cirque de Boudoir written in title case, not capitals (public/events.json, past-events.json; the upcoming ones said CIRQUE DU BOUDOIR).

R14-55. MM-RYTM one-shots (2026-10-03, Mika: "les meilleurs samples oneshot du monde ... un kick de balle de tennis qui frappe fort, un snare qui eblouit avec une petite gate reverb pas trop intense"). The ten voices (and CHopen) are now sample playback: one AudioBufferSourceNode per hit, playbackRate = TONE pitch factor (sampler style), STRETCH picks the sample rendered for that duration factor (rounded to 1/8 of a factor 4, shotKey), OH keeps its choke gate. The samples are computed in JS (audio/shotsdsp.ts), no files: everything at 4x the context rate, then a 127-tap Kaiser FIR decimator (beta 8); layers per voice, soft saturation, peak-normalized to SHOT_PEAK (dB, above 0 allowed). BD: sine 380 -> 50 Hz (11 ms then 55 ms), tanh 3.2, a 1.15 kHz 'pok' (7 ms) and 2.2 kHz noise (3 ms), body 130 ms. SD: skin 185 / 330 / 540 Hz, wires (noise HP 1.2 kHz, +4 dB at 4.5 kHz, +1.5 dB air, LP 15 kHz), crack (2.5 kHz, 2 ms), then a gated reverb (8-line FDN, stereo, RT60 1.1 s, -10 dB, open 130 ms, closed in 40 ms). CP: four hands (0, 9.5, 19, 31 ms, jittered), 1.25 kHz band, small room. Hats and cymbal: the 808 metallic squares, band and high pass, noise veil, mild air, LP 16 kHz; CY stereo. Toms, RS (980 / 290 Hz), PC (200 Hz conga) layered and saturated. Round robin: SD 3, CH 4, CHopen 2, OH 3, CP 3, RS 2, CY 2, PC 2 variants, never the same twice in a row. Rendering runs in a Web Worker (audio/shots.worker.ts): prewarmed at 48 kHz once the intro is done (no AudioContext), then at the context rate; a miss takes the nearest cached sample (STRETCH, other rate, other variant) and queues the exact one; offline renders are synchronous and reproducible. Cost: 24 samples, 248 ms CPU in the worker (production build), no main-thread long task.
R14-56. Machine balance (2026-10-03, Mika: "le MM-RYTM sonne moins fort que l'ARP"). Measured offline after the shared compressor (it adds automatic makeup gain): above 500 Hz the drums were 6 dB under the arpeggio (-26.8 vs -20.9 dB) while their LUFS was higher (sub of the kick). Kit peaks raised (SHOT_PEAK), kick denser (tanh 3.2) and 1.5 dB lower in peak, synth VOLUME_K 1.15 -> 0.75. Result: drums -24.5 dB above 500 Hz, -11.4 LUFS; arpeggio -23.7 dB above 500 Hz, -16.7 LUFS; drum peaks held at -1 dBFS by the limiter.

R14-57. MM-ARP like the Dreadbox Typhon (2026-10-03, Mika: "un bouton que quand on tourne ca morph les waveformes et un bouton FM qui module le tout"). WAVE 1 and WAVE 2 are continuous morphs (VoyKnob.morph: the value is no longer rounded, the steps are landmarks): the engine crossfades the two neighbouring shapes at the smoothed position (15 ms), all shapes phase-aligned on a +sin fundamental (triangle shifted a quarter, falling saw), so the fundamental never collapses mid-morph (220 Hz fundamental across WAVE 1 positions 0 to 5 in half steps: 52.7, 52.2, 51.1, 50.4, 49.4, 52.5, 54.7, 50.8, 46.7, 45.9, 48.9 dB, no dip). The silk ring lights both neighbours by their share (light theme: the dominant one orange), redrawn per twentieth of a step; LCD and twins read 'SAW>SQUARE 40%'; the Dock key still steps to the next exact shape; the wheel turns WAVE continuously, arrow keys step shape by shape. The FM operator now modulates both oscillators (OSC 2 alone, FM 100 %: rich harmonics at 2x and 3x instead of a pure 220 Hz).
R14-58. MM-ARP analog quality (2026-10-03, Mika: "la qualite exceptionnelle ... des basses qui sonnent super bien et des mediums aigus cristallins, presque du analog"). Ladder filter with saturation inside each of the four stages (Mystran's cheap method: each stage takes the mean slope of its tanh between its input and state, the ladder is then solved linearly without delay; fast rational tanh, NL_A 0.6), replacing the single input tanh: loud signals round off, resonance self-limits while singing. Stage slopes, cutoff and pitch increments computed once per context sample (not per oversampled sample): 8 s of arpeggio render in 207 ms offline (314 ms before). Bass kept with resonance: BASS_KEEP 0.45 (0.3). Voice variance per note: cutoff +/- 2.8 %, envelope rates +/- 5 %, tuning +/- 1.5 cent. Stereo: each note panned a little, alternately left and right (SPREAD 0.22, L/R correlation 0.96 dry), two decimation chains. Output gain 1.25 (1.5): the default arpeggio stays at -19.7 LUFS (dry, offline); resonance 0.95 about 2.4 LU louder than before, a deep bass note 0.6 LU fuller. All extremes (res 1, drive 1, noise 1, FM 1): no NaN, peak -14 dBFS.

R14-59. MM-ARP RANDOM changes the whole patch (2026-10-04, Mika: "tous les parametres de l'arp, des oscillators, filtre, adsr, vraiment tout"): voyager/random.ts draws every knob but VOLUME in musical ranges (rate mostly 1/16 and 1/8, octave -1..+1, WAVE pure shape half the time else mid-morph, FM 45 % of the time with harmonic ratios, cutoff 0.25-0.75, res 0.05-0.75, attacks mostly short, effects as before), plus a new progression; LCD 'RANDOM PATCH'. 2000 draws: every knob but VOLUME touched, all values in 0..1; 8 offline renders: -12.5 to -18 LUFS, peaks under -1.5 dBFS, no NaN.
R14-60. Mobile menu, full screen (2026-10-04, Mika: "faut vraiment quelque chose de plus beau en mobile"): under the header, the whole screen in the page colour (cream #F6F1E7 in light, ink in dark, the header takes the same colour while open); the five pages as big numbered titles (01 to 05, 30 to 40 px, hairlines, an arrow each, the open one in orange); GOODIES MERCH STUDIO as pills under 'Under the hood'; six socials under 'Follow' (Instagram, SoundCloud, Spotify, Apple Music, YouTube, Bandcamp; brand colour on touch); at the foot OPEN THE MACHINE (full orange, 56 px) then Reset view and Dark / Light. Rows arrive one after the other (35 ms stagger), none with reduced motion.
R14-61. Cursor (2026-10-04, Mika: "comme quand on hover un lien, je veux pas les deux fleches"): the hand (pointer) over and while turning any encoder or MM-ARP knob; the resize arrows are gone.
R14-62. Listening quality for in-ear monitors (2026-10-04, Mika: "je plug des Sennheiser IE900, la meilleure des meilleures qualites"). (1) The MM-ARP no longer enters the drum compressor (it ducked the arpeggio by several dB on every kick): it joins the analyser after it (limiter, master); VOLUME_K 0.75 -> 1.03 keeps the balance (above 500 Hz: arpeggio -24.2 dB, drums -24.5 dB). (2) TONE on one-shots: a pitched hit is rendered at its pitch (rendered at sr / pf, played at sr, rate 1, key in thousandths of a semitone) instead of being resampled by the browser's linear interpolation; the nearest cached sample covers the wait. (3) Chorus (drum bus, drum voices, MM-ARP Juno) as an AudioWorklet (audio/chorus.worklet.js) reading its modulated delay with 16-point Kaiser-windowed sinc interpolation (512 phases) instead of the DelayNode; same settings and panning formulas (StereoPanner mono and stereo); branches built before the module loads fade out and are rebuilt on it (20 ms). Measured on a single bus voice, mono sine: DelayNode wet level -0.8 / -1.7 / -3.0 / -3.9 dB at 8 / 12 / 16 / 19 kHz with 0.7 / 1.5 / 2.8 / 3.9 dB flutter; sinc: flat (0.0 dB) and 0.01 dB flutter up to 16 kHz, -0.2 dB and 0.24 dB at 19 kHz.

R14-63. Desktop menu like the phone's (2026-10-04, Mika: "le menu desktop aussi plus beau comme le mobile"). The inline link bar (11 px) is replaced by a MENU pill (word + three bars, CLOSE and a cross when open, orange outline on hover) that opens the same full-screen menu, now one component (ui/MenuSheet.tsx, variant 'mobile' or 'desk'). Desktop layout: the five pages on the left as numbered titles 40 to 96 px (6.2 vw / 10.5 vh), hairlines, on hover the title slides 14 px and turns orange and its arrow arrives; on the right, Under the hood pills, Follow socials, OPEN THE MACHINE (60 px) then Reset view and Dark / Light; both columns centred together, content 1320 px wide at most; the header takes the page colour while open. Escape closes and returns focus to MENU; the menu button carries data-press-button (focus back after the press kit viewer). OPEN THE MACHINE acts on the machine in use (hoodMachine), on both layouts.

R14-64. Clap softened (2026-10-04, Mika: "le clap est vraiment trop intense"): CP peak 3.5 -> -2 dB (5.5 dB lower), saturation drive 3 -> 2, the 2.6 kHz bump +3 -> +1.5 dB, its room -13 -> -16 dB. A clap on 2 and 4 now sits 12.7 LU under the four-on-the-floor kick (offline).
R14-65. MM-RYTM RANDOM, every 4x4 that holds up (2026-10-04, Mika: "je veux pas que de la house, je veux tout ce qui tient la route en 4x4", "le tone, le volume"). audio/beats.ts draws a style (never the same twice in a row): HOUSE (audio/house.ts grammar), TECH HOUSE, TECHNO, MINIMAL, INDIE DANCE, PROG, ELECTRO, each with its grammar (kick always on the four beats, sometimes a soft pickup; backbeat by clap, snare or rim per style; closed hats never on an open-hat step, the choke would cut it). Then each voice's TONE and VOLUME (VOICE FX tone, level) in safe per-voice ranges (kick tone -0.15..0.12 and volume 0.76..0.86, hats tone -0.1..0.35, toms, rim and conga up to +/-0.45; one voice in three keeps its TONE centred); the other voice effects, GLOBAL FX and tempo stay. LCD: 'RANDOM TECHNO' etc. 700 draws: every style, kick on the four beats 700/700, no invalid step, no closed hat under an open one; densities 11 (minimal) to 41 hits; offline renders -10.4 to -11.9 LUFS, peaks under -1.2 dBFS.

R14-66. MM-ARP oscillator levels, filter modes, MOD (2026-10-04, Mika: "il me faut les volumes des oscillators", "la generation au random sort des trucs un peu tout le temps pareil, trouve quelque chose a rajouter dans la synthese"). (1) OSC 1 and OSC 2 replace MIX: each oscillator's level (gain 0.88 x knob^2, default 0.84 = the old centred MIX), smoothed. (2) Filter MODE replaces SLOPE: a 4-position switch (LP 24, LP 12, BP, HP; 150 deg throw, marks 24 12 BP HP, a tap goes to the next and loops), the ladder outputs mixed like an Oberheim Xpander with the 4-pole feedback kept (BP = 2 (y1 - y2), HP = u - 2 y1 + y2), 15 ms crossfade between modes, bass kept in low pass only; BP x1.25 / (1 + 0.12 k), HP x1.7 / (1 + 0.1 k) so the default arpeggio sits at LP24 -17.3, LP12 -17.3, BP -18.4, HP -20.2 LUFS (offline, dry). (3) MOD, a tempo-locked LFO shared by the notes (SPEED 1/16 to 4 bars, bpm sent with the parameters; restarts on the first note after STOP), SHAPE triangle / saw / square / sample and hold (one draw per cycle, 2 ms smoothing), TARGET wave morph (+/-2 steps), cutoff (+/-3 octaves), FM (+/-0.6), pitch (+/-1 semitone) or wave and cutoff, DEPTH. LFO to cutoff at 1/4, 130 BPM: brightness swings about 10 dB once per beat. RANDOM draws the new controls (mode with a cutoff range per mode, levels, MOD half the time). Layout: desktop panel on three rows (-0.74, 0.46, 1.64; the bottom of the panel was free): OSC (WAVE 1 WAVE 2 TUNE 2 / OSC 1 OSC 2 NOISE / FM RATIO FINE), FILTER (CUTOFF big in the middle; RES, ENV AMT, MODE), FILTER EG / AMP EG / MOD (SPEED SHAPE TARGET DEPTH, its title between rows), EFFECTS, OUTPUT; rules down to the third row. Phone: knobs at panel scale 1.0 (1.15), eight per row (pitch 0.843, group gap 0.3): OSC (WAVE 1 WAVE 2 TUNE 2 FM RATIO FINE), FILTER (CUTOFF RES ENV AMT NOISE MODE) + COLOR, FILTER EG + MOD, AMP EG + MIXER (OSC 1 OSC 2) + SPACE; GLIDE joins the deck ARPEGGIATOR (seven knobs); CUTOFF without graduations on the phone. No NaN at every extreme; 8 s of arpeggio still render in about 180 ms offline.

R14-67. MM-ARP knob hierarchy (2026-10-04, Mika, with a photo of the Typhon: "un effort de design de placement de knobs avec des plus gros et des moins gros, en mobile et en desktop"). Five sizes (voyager/theme.ts knobSize): xl CUTOFF (1.5 desktop, 1.6 phone), l WAVE 1, WAVE 2 and VOLUME (1.18 / 1.15), m what shapes the sound (OSC 1, OSC 2, FM, RES, ENV AMT, DEPTH, deck RATE and GATE; 1.0 / 0.95), s the fine settings (envelopes, effects, MOD speed, shape, target, TUNE 2, FINE, RATIO, NOISE; 0.8), sw the MODE switch (0.62). Big knobs keep their 0-10 graduations; medium and small ones get a printed 270 deg arc just outside the skirt (Typhon rings): teal rgba(78, 196, 205, 0.62) on the dark machine, silk ink at 0.3 on the light one. Layouts become compositions: desktop OSCILLATORS = WAVE 1 / WAVE 2 large, the mixer under them (OSC 1, NOISE, OSC 2, each level under its wave), FM + RATIO under OSC 1 and TUNE 2 + FINE under OSC 2; FILTER = CUTOFF xl on top, RES and ENV AMT, MODE; FILTER EG / AMP EG / MOD rows of small knobs (DEPTH medium); EFFECTS four small; OUTPUT VOLUME large (rows -0.72, 0.66, 1.72). Phone: OSCILLATORS (WAVE 1, WAVE 2 large, then TUNE 2 over FINE and FM over RATIO), FILTER (CUTOFF xl, RES, ENV AMT, MODE) + MIXER (OSC 1, OSC 2, NOISE), FILTER EG + MOD, AMP EG + EFFECTS; group brackets now span every knob of a composed group. A geometric check (footprints with rings, graduations, arcs and switch marks; labels, titles, brackets, rules, panel bounds) reports no overlap on either layout.

R14-68. R14-73. MM-ARP sequence, editable note by note (2026-10-04, Mika: "pouvoir modifier les notes qui sont generees", the arpeggio run by RUN or the space bar). voyager/seq.ts: a note is a degree of F# natural minor above the root of the chord that plays (0 root, 2 third, 4 fifth, 6 seventh, 7 octave, up to 20) or a rest, so one sequence follows the progression and stays in key (chords.ts degreeMidi, checked equal to arpSequence for the 8 chords, 1 to 3 octaves, every mode). AUTO = the sequence MODE, RANGE and NOTES make (same notes as before; RAND still draws each note, the lane shows the last draws); EDIT = Mika's, 1 to 16 steps; touching an AUTO note copies it to EDIT first; AUTO goes back (EDIT keeps its sequence and recalls it); RANDOM, MODE, RANGE and NOTES return to AUTO; kept in localStorage (mm.v4.voyager.seq.1). arp.ts plays it (rests skip the note, glide resumes from the last one) and logs every step for the playhead (posAt). UI ui/SeqLane.tsx: one bar per step, its height is its note; drag to draw (steps crossed follow the line), the note name under a bar toggles a rest; guides on the chord tones (stronger on roots); header chord, AUTO / EDIT, STEPS - n +, RAND tag; keyboard: each bar a slider (up/down, Page = octave, Home/End, Delete = rest, Enter = toggle, left/right between bars). Desktop: a panel under the MM-ARP when it is in use (closed, no page open), the machine framed above it (Stage.setInset('voy'), the MM-DECKS playlist now goes through the same setInset('dj'); a new stage after an appearance change gets the panel height again). Phone: the Dock gets two pages, CONTROLS and SEQUENCE (kept in mm.v4.vdock.page; the tab follows the measured Dock height). LCD line 1: 1/16 SEQ 8 STEPS in EDIT. Checked, sound muted (?mute=1): drawn line 19..1 interpolated across steps, notes played = the drawn sequence (86 80 74 68 62 56), playhead over every step, a rest skips its note, AUTO back to the F#m arpeggio (54 57 61 66 69 73), EDIT recalls, MODE to RAND returns to AUTO with live draws, 16 steps fit 375 px (20 px per column), console clean.

R14-74. MM-DECKS reads AIFF (2026-10-04, Mika: "unable to decode audio data, resous le probleme rapidement", a folder of his own tracks). Chrome, Brave and Edge do not decode AIFF (nor ALAC); dj/decode.ts decodeAudio: the browser first (on a copy of the bytes), then our parser: AIFF and AIFF-C (PCM 8 to 32 bits big-endian, 'sowt' little-endian, 'raw ', fl32 / fl64) and the WAV the browser rejects (RF64 / BW64, WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE, sizes 0xFFFFFFFF or past the end; PCM and float). The buffer keeps the file's rate (playback resamples). What still fails says why: ALAC ("Safari does, or convert to AIFF"), protected iTunes AAC (DRM), otherwise the browser message plus the sniffed format. Used by the deck load (engine.ts) and the crate analysis (crate.ts, one mono channel for the BPM). Checked: AIFF 16 / 24 bits, AIFF-C sowt and fl32, RF64 extensible 24 bits decode to the exact samples; a 3 s AIFF loads on deck A with its BPM (120), nothing played. The browser's "Upload 171 files to this site?" on + FOLDER is its own wording for a local folder read: nothing is sent; dropping the folder from the Finder onto the playlist avoids that prompt.

R14-78. MM-RYTM SOLO as a mode (2026-10-04, Mika: "je voulais choisir BD et c'est CP qui se met en SOLO"). R14-79. MM-RYTM voice DECAY and time-stretch STRETCH (2026-10-04, Mika: "au lieu de Stretch dans les voices mets Decay pour gerer le decay des oneshots ; laisse Stretch dans les globales mais qu'il agisse comme un Timestretch, rappelle-toi Impulse"). VOICE FX STRETCH becomes DECAY (vdecay, unipolar, default 1 = the whole tail): below 1 the hit keeps 4 ms of attack then decays exponentially (time constant 12 ms at 0 to about 0.7 s), the source stops once silent. Global STRETCH (still -1..1, x0.25 to x4) no longer scales the envelopes: the one-shot is rendered at its natural length then time-stretched at the same pitch by granular overlap-add (audio/stretch.ts: 40 ms Hann grains, 75 % overlap, normalised by the window sum, the first 6 ms kept original and crossfaded), once per sample in the worker. Checked offline: OH 0.16 / 0.31 / 0.605 / 1.20 / 2.38 s at -1 / -0.5 / 0 / 0.5 / 1, peak kept within 0.5 dB; DECAY 1 / 0.5 / 0.1: 0.605 / 0.40 / 0.11 s.

R14-87. MM-RYTM indie dance kick (2026-10-04, Mika: "pour le kick, quelque chose de moins intense, un kick de indie dance"). audio/shotsdsp.ts bd: rounder and warmer instead of the tennis ball: a sine falling from 200 to 52 Hz (9 ms sweep, then a slow 45 ms remainder; was 380 to 50 Hz), soft saturation (tanh 1.5 normalised; was 3.2), a discreet attack bump, a felt click (band noise around 1.6 kHz, 2 ms; was a 1.15 kHz "pok" and a 2.2 kHz breath), 120 ms tail; SHOT_PEAK BD -0.5 -> -2.5 dB. The LCD says RANDOM BASSLINE etc. Levels kept close (VOLUME never moves): 6 offline draws per style, mean RMS -16.6 to -19.5 dB (PLUCK given more amp sustain, LEAD's oscillators lowered under its FM). 300 draws: every style, no repeat; bass lines down to F#1 (46 Hz).

R14-91. MM-ARP TWEAKS under the hood (2026-10-04, Mika, with a photo of the Mini V oscillator panel: "un bouton pour qu'on n'ait pas de probleme de phase dans les low ; ca dephase pour l'impression d'analog, mais c'est un peu trop intense ; dans OPEN, d'autres boutons pour changer certaines choses, en mobile et en desktop ; enleve les liens du site et mets des tweaks a la place ; je veux un super synth"). The MM-ARP board loses its site chips (Pcb option chips: false: no chip bodies, footprints, silk names, hotspots; the MM-RYTM board keeps them; the menu's GOODIES / MERCH / STUDIO now always open the MM-RYTM hood). In their place voyager/tweaks.ts: a plate in the lid colour on four aluminium standoffs (0.5 above the board, over the low parts), child of pcb.parts so it grows with them when the hood opens, turned back on the phone so it reads upright; the same Moog knobs as the face, names above, 0 to 10 graduations, end words below (FREE / 360, STABLE / LOOSE, MONO / WIDE, OFF / 300 HZ, 0 / FULL, FLAT / HARD), SYNC a two-position switch (OFF / ON), a TWEAKS title block. Desktop two rows of four cells (knobs x1.3), phone four rows of two (x1.3). Hotspots vk-<id> on the plate, live only once the hood is open (like the chips before), twins after the face knobs (focusable when live). Seven new knobs (section 'tweak', stored and in presets; an older preset without them loads their defaults, the sound of before):
- PHASE: FREE (default: free-running oscillators, a random phase for each fresh voice, as before) or 0 to 360 deg: every note restarts its three oscillators at that phase (only on a voice below 2 % of its envelope, no click), so a bass hits the same at every note. Offline, drift 0: two notes differ by +3 dB in FREE, -114 dB at 0 deg.
- DRIFT: the analog part, 0 stable to 2x (default 1 = before): slow oscillator drift and the per-note cutoff / envelope / tuning spread.
- WIDTH: stereo spread of the notes, 0 centred to 0.44 (default 0.22 = before).
- BASS MONO: OFF or 40 to 300 Hz. audio/synth.ts splits the engine with a Linkwitz-Riley crossover (two Butterworth biquads each side, the halves sum flat): the top goes through CHORUS, DELAY and REVERB as before, the bottom is summed to mono and goes dry to the output at VOLUME. OFF parks the crossover at 10 Hz (everything through the top). Offline (chorus 0.6, width 0.8): side energy under 90 Hz -8 dB relative to mid at OFF, -29 dB at 120 Hz, -37 dB at 170 Hz; the chorus used to hollow the lows around 100 Hz (F#2 -3.4 dB vs dry), BASS MONO gives them back at the dry level.
- KEY TRACK: cutoff follows the note, 0 to 1 (default 0.5 = before).
- ACCENT: depth of the arpeggio accents (arp.ts), 0 flat, 0.5 before, 1 double.
- SYNC: OSC 2 hard synced to OSC 1 (reset at OSC 1's wrap, sub-sample exact): no beating between them, TUNE 2 becomes timbre.
RANDOM sets them per style: BASSLINE phase 0 deg, drift 0.1-0.3, width 0.1-0.3, BASS MONO 135-170 Hz; ACID phase 0 deg, accents 0.7-1, key track 0.6-0.85, BASS MONO 105-150 Hz; LEAD SYNC one time in three, DARK one in four; the rest phase FREE and BASS MONO 80-120 Hz six times in ten. Checked muted: renders without NaN for every tweak, SYNC within 0.7 dB, a real drag on PHASE, desktop and phone screenshots open, menu GOODIES with the MM-ARP in use opens the MM-RYTM hood with its trace.
R14-92. MM-DECKS: a track from a past visit plays again (2026-10-04, Mika: "dans un DECK, quand je clique sur une track d'un dossier que j'avais rajoute avant, ca ne fait rien ; c'est un dossier d'iCloud Drive, les tracks sont telechargees"). Those tracks came in THIS VISIT ONLY (or from a browser without folder linking: Safari, Firefox, Brave by default): only their tags, BPM and cues were kept, and their row was disabled. dj/TrackBrowser.tsx: the row now says "Tap, then pick its folder again" and is live; a tap asks for the folder (Chrome and Edge: the folder picker, the folder becomes LINKED for good; elsewhere: the folder chooser). When the folder comes back (picked or dropped), the track found by its fingerprint (name, size, date) goes straight onto that deck, then the folder is kept on the device without another question (copied when there is room, otherwise for the visit); a folder without that track says THIS TRACK IS NOT IN THAT FOLDER. Checked muted (folder chooser path, Brave-like): a past-visit WAV reloaded, tapped, its folder given back: loaded on deck A (not playing), now stored, no longer greyed, its folder kept.

Readable TWEAKS and the decks' logotype (2026-10-04, Mika: "on ne voit plus le logo type" ; "super ce que tu as fait sur les Tweaks mais on voit rien, fais les boutons un peu plus gros et surtout les titres, sans trop exagerer").
